     1→"""Field mapping generation tool for grounded entities.
     2→
     3→Per user decision: "Generate suggested field_mappings during grounding (ground node)"
     4→Per CONTEXT.md: "Criteria should be decomposed per AutoCriteria pattern: separate
     5→Entity, Operator, Value, Unit, Time"
     6→Per user decision: "MedGemma and Gemini collaborate: Gemini uses MedGemma as
     7→medical expert"
     8→
     9→Uses Gemini to generate suggested field mappings for the grounded entity +
    10→criterion text. These are best-effort suggestions — reviewer can edit in UI.
    11→"""
    12→
    13→from __future__ import annotations
    14→
    15→import logging
    16→from typing import Any, Literal
    17→
    18→from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator, model_validator
    19→
    20→from protocol_processor.prompts import render_template
    21→from protocol_processor.schemas.grounding import EntityGroundingResult
    22→from protocol_processor.tools.gemini_utils import (
    23→    create_structured_llm,
    24→    parse_structured_output,
    25→)
    26→
    27→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    28→
    29→# Mapping from legacy/LLM relation operators to the frontend's RelationOperator set
    30→_RELATION_MAP: dict[str, str] = {
    31→    "has": "contains",
    32→    "is": "=",
    33→    "not": "not_contains",
    34→    "==": "=",
    35→    "range": "within",
    36→}
    37→
    38→
    39→def _normalize_relation(rel: str) -> str:
    40→    """Normalize a relation operator to the frontend's accepted set.
    41→
    42→    Maps legacy operators (has, is, not, ==, range) to the standard set:
    43→    =, !=, >, >=, <, <=, within, not_in_last, contains, not_contains.
    44→    """
    45→    return _RELATION_MAP.get(rel, rel)
    46→
    47→
    48→class FieldMappingValue(BaseModel):
    49→    """Typed value object for field mappings with type discriminator.
    50→
    51→    Supports three value shapes:
    52→    - standard: single value + unit (e.g. HbA1c < 7%)
    53→    - range: min/max + unit (e.g. Age 18-65 years)
    54→    - temporal: duration + unit (e.g. within 6 months)
    55→    """
    56→
    57→    type: Literal["standard", "range", "temporal"] = Field(
    58→        description="Value type discriminator"
    59→    )
    60→    value: str | None = Field(
    61→        default=None, description="Value for standard type (e.g. '7')"
    62→    )
    63→    unit: str | None = Field(
    64→        default=None, description="Unit of measurement (e.g. '%', 'mg/dL', 'months')"
    65→    )
    66→    min: str | None = Field(default=None, description="Minimum value for range type")
    67→    max: str | None = Field(default=None, description="Maximum value for range type")
    68→    duration: str | None = Field(
    69→        default=None, description="Duration value for temporal type"
    70→    )
    71→
    72→    @model_validator(mode="after")
    73→    def truncate_long_strings(self) -> "FieldMappingValue":
    74→        """Guard against LLM repetition loops producing absurdly long values."""
    75→        _max = 200
    76→        for attr in ("value", "unit", "min", "max", "duration"):
    77→            val = getattr(self, attr)
    78→            if isinstance(val, str) and len(val) > _max:
    79→                setattr(self, attr, val[:_max])
    80→        return self
    81→
    82→
    83→# The set of valid relation operators accepted by the frontend
    84→RelationOperator = Literal[
    85→    "=", "!=", ">", ">=", "<", "<=", "within", "not_in_last", "contains", "not_contains"
    86→]
    87→
    88→
    89→class FieldMappingItem(BaseModel):
    90→    """A single AutoCriteria field mapping decomposition.
    91→
    92→    Per the AutoCriteria pattern, each criterion is decomposed into
    93→    separate Entity, Operator, Value, Unit, and Time components.
    94→    """
    95→
    96→    entity: str = Field(description="The medical entity name (e.g. 'HbA1c')")
    97→    relation: RelationOperator = Field(
    98→        description="The logical operator/relation (e.g. '<', '>', '=', 'contains')"
    99→    )
   100→    value: FieldMappingValue = Field(
   101→        description="Typed value object with type discriminator"
   102→    )
   103→    unit: str | None = Field(
   104→        default=None,
   105→        description="Optional unit of measurement (e.g. '%', 'mg/dL', 'years')",
   106→    )
   107→    value_concept_id: str | None = Field(
   108→        default=None,
   109→        description="OMOP concept ID for categorical values",
   110→    )
   111→    value_concept_system: str | None = Field(
   112→        default=None,
   113→        description="Terminology system for value_concept_id (e.g. 'SNOMED', 'OMOP')",
   114→    )
   115→
   116→    @field_validator("relation", mode="before")
   117→    @classmethod
   118→    def normalize_relation(cls, v: str) -> str:
   119→        """Normalize LLM-generated relation operators before Literal validation."""
   120→        if isinstance(v, str):
   121→            return _normalize_relation(v)
   122→        return v
   123→
   124→
   125→class FieldMappingResponse(BaseModel):
   126→    """Gemini structured output for field mappings."""
   127→
   128→    mappings: list[FieldMappingItem] = Field(
   129→        default_factory=list,
   130→        description="List of AutoCriteria field mapping decompositions",
   131→    )
   132→
   133→
   134→async def generate_field_mappings(
   135→    entity: EntityGroundingResult,
   136→    criterion_text: str,
   137→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   138→    """Generate suggested field mappings for a grounded entity.
   139→
   140→    Uses Gemini to decompose the criterion text into AutoCriteria field mappings:
   141→    Entity, Operator, Value, Unit, Time components for each discrete condition.
   142→
   143→    This is a best-effort suggestion — reviewer can edit in the UI. Errors
   144→    are logged and an empty list returned (not propagated as failures).
   145→
   146→    Args:
   147→        entity: Grounded EntityGroundingResult with code and preferred term.
   148→        criterion_text: Full criterion text for context.
   149→
   150→    Returns:
   151→        List of field mapping dicts with keys: entity, relation, value,
   152→        entity_code, entity_system, omop_concept_id, entity_type.
   153→        Empty list if generation fails.
   154→    """
   155→    if not criterion_text:
   156→        return []
   157→
   158→    structured_llm = create_structured_llm(FieldMappingResponse)
   159→    if structured_llm is None:
   160→        return []
   161→
   162→    try:
   163→        # Build a context-rich prompt for field mapping generation
   164→        grounded_term = entity.preferred_term or entity.entity_text
   165→        code_context = ""
   166→        if entity.selected_code and entity.selected_system:
   167→            system = entity.selected_system.upper()
   168→            code_context = f"(grounded to {system} code: {entity.selected_code})"
   169→
   170→        prompt = render_template(
   171→            "field_mapping.jinja2",
   172→            grounded_term=grounded_term,
   173→            code_context=code_context,
   174→            criterion_text=criterion_text,
   175→        )
   176→
   177→        from protocol_processor.tracing import llm_span
   178→
   179→        with llm_span("gemini_field_mapping") as llm:
   180→            llm.set_request(prompt)
   181→            result = await structured_llm.ainvoke(prompt)
   182→            llm.set_response(str(result))
   183→
   184→        response = parse_structured_output(result, FieldMappingResponse)
   185→
   186→        mappings = [
   187→            {
   188→                "entity": m.entity,
   189→                "relation": m.relation,
   190→                "value": m.value.model_dump(exclude_none=True),
   191→                "entity_code": entity.selected_code,
   192→                "entity_system": entity.selected_system,
   193→                "omop_concept_id": entity.omop_concept_id,
   194→                "entity_type": entity.entity_type,
   195→                "value_concept_id": m.value_concept_id,
   196→                "value_concept_system": m.value_concept_system,
   197→            }
   198→            for m in response.mappings
   199→        ]
   200→
   201→        logger.info(
   202→            "Generated %d field mapping(s) for entity '%s'",
   203→            len(mappings),
   204→            entity.entity_text[:50],
   205→        )
   206→        return mappings
   207→
   208→    except Exception as e:
   209→        logger.warning(
   210→            "Field mapping generation failed for entity '%s': %s",
   211→            entity.entity_text[:50],
   212→            e,
   213→            exc_info=True,
   214→        )
   215→        # Best-effort: return empty list on failure
   216→        return []
   217→

     1→"""Unit and value normalizer: UCUM lookup with OMOP concept ID resolution.
     2→
     3→Queries the OMOP vocabulary database for UCUM unit codes, categorical value
     4→concepts, and ordinal scale grades. All lookups are cached via lru_cache
     5→for performance.
     6→
     7→No LLM calls — pure DB lookup with alias expansion and case-insensitive
     8→matching.  Returns (None, None) for unrecognized inputs; never raises.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→import re
    15→from functools import lru_cache
    16→from typing import Any
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→_ORDINAL_PREFIX_RE = re.compile(
    21→    r"^(?:grade|stage|class|score|level)\s*",
    22→    re.IGNORECASE,
    23→)
    24→
    25→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    26→# Clinical knowledge: ordinal scale alias -> canonical scale name
    27→# (This mapping is not in OMOP — it's clinical domain knowledge about
    28→# which entity names refer to which performance/grading scale.)
    29→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    30→
    31→ORDINAL_SCALE_ALIASES: dict[str, str] = {
    32→    "ecog": "ecog",
    33→    "ecog ps": "ecog",
    34→    "ecog performance status": "ecog",
    35→    "eastern cooperative oncology group": "ecog",
    36→    "who performance status": "ecog",
    37→    "zubrod score": "ecog",
    38→    "karnofsky": "karnofsky",
    39→    "kps": "karnofsky",
    40→    "karnofsky performance status": "karnofsky",
    41→    "karnofsky performance scale": "karnofsky",
    42→    "nyha": "nyha",
    43→    "nyha class": "nyha",
    44→    "nyha functional class": "nyha",
    45→    "nyha classification": "nyha",
    46→    "new york heart association": "nyha",
    47→    "asa": "asa",
    48→    "asa physical status": "asa",
    49→    "asa ps": "asa",
    50→    "asa class": "asa",
    51→    "asa classification": "asa",
    52→    "asa score": "asa",
    53→    "american society of anesthesiologists": "asa",
    54→}
    55→
    56→# Unit concept ID for ordinal scores — always 8527 ({score})
    57→_ORDINAL_UNIT_CONCEPT_ID = 8527
    58→
    59→
    60→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    61→# DB-backed unit normalization
    62→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    63→
    64→
    65→@lru_cache(maxsize=512)
    66→def _cached_ucum_lookup(unit_key: str) -> tuple[str | None, int | None]:
    67→    """Cached wrapper around _lookup_ucum_unit (keyed on lowercased unit text)."""
    68→    from protocol_processor.tools.omop_mapper import _get_omop_engine, _lookup_ucum_unit
    69→
    70→    try:
    71→        engine = _get_omop_engine()
    72→    except RuntimeError:
    73→        logger.debug("OMOP_VOCAB_URL not set — unit lookup unavailable")
    74→        return (None, None)
    75→    return _lookup_ucum_unit(engine, unit_key)
    76→
    77→
    78→def normalize_unit(unit_text: str | None) -> tuple[str | None, int | None]:
    79→    """Normalize a unit string to its UCUM code and OMOP unit_concept_id.
    80→
    81→    Args:
    82→        unit_text: Raw unit text (e.g. "mg/dL", "%", "years").
    83→
    84→    Returns:
    85→        Tuple of (ucum_code, omop_unit_concept_id), or (None, None)
    86→        if the input is None, empty, or unrecognized.
    87→    """
    88→    if unit_text is not None and not isinstance(unit_text, str):
    89→        unit_text = str(unit_text)
    90→    if not unit_text or not unit_text.strip():
    91→        return (None, None)
    92→
    93→    key = unit_text.strip().lower()
    94→    return _cached_ucum_lookup(key)
    95→
    96→
    97→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    98→# DB-backed categorical value normalization
    99→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   100→
   101→
   102→@lru_cache(maxsize=128)
   103→def _cached_value_lookup(value_key: str) -> tuple[str | None, int | None]:
   104→    """Cached wrapper around _lookup_value_concept (keyed on lowercased value text)."""
   105→    from protocol_processor.tools.omop_mapper import (
   106→        _get_omop_engine,
   107→        _lookup_value_concept,
   108→    )
   109→
   110→    try:
   111→        engine = _get_omop_engine()
   112→    except RuntimeError:
   113→        logger.debug("OMOP_VOCAB_URL not set — value lookup unavailable")
   114→        return (None, None)
   115→    return _lookup_value_concept(engine, value_key)
   116→
   117→
   118→def normalize_value(
   119→    value_text: str | None,
   120→) -> tuple[str | None, int | None]:
   121→    """Normalize a categorical value to its SNOMED code and OMOP concept ID.
   122→
   123→    Args:
   124→        value_text: Raw value text (e.g. "positive", "negative", "normal").
   125→
   126→    Returns:
   127→        Tuple of (normalized_text, omop_value_concept_id), or (None, None)
   128→        if the input is None, empty, or unrecognized.
   129→    """
   130→    if value_text is not None and not isinstance(value_text, str):
   131→        value_text = str(value_text)
   132→    if not value_text or not value_text.strip():
   133→        return (None, None)
   134→
   135→    key = value_text.strip().lower()
   136→    return _cached_value_lookup(key)
   137→
   138→
   139→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   140→# Ordinal scale normalization
   141→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   142→
   143→
   144→def _match_ordinal_scale(entity_text: str) -> str | None:
   145→    """Match entity text to an ordinal scale key.
   146→
   147→    Uses three strategies in order:
   148→    1. Exact alias match (case-insensitive)
   149→    2. Entity text is a substring of an alias
   150→    3. An alias is a substring of entity text
   151→
   152→    Returns:
   153→        Scale key (e.g. "ecog") or None if no match.
   154→    """
   155→    key = entity_text.strip().lower()
   156→
   157→    # Strategy 1: exact alias match
   158→    if key in ORDINAL_SCALE_ALIASES:
   159→        return ORDINAL_SCALE_ALIASES[key]
   160→
   161→    # Strategy 2 & 3: bidirectional substring containment
   162→    for alias, scale_key in ORDINAL_SCALE_ALIASES.items():
   163→        if key in alias or alias in key:
   164→            return scale_key
   165→
   166→    return None
   167→
   168→
   169→def normalize_ordinal_value(
   170→    value_text: str | None,
   171→    entity_text: str | None = None,
   172→) -> tuple[int | None, int | None] | None:
   173→    """Entity-context-aware ordinal value normalization.
   174→
   175→    Returns:
   176→        (value_concept_id, unit_concept_id) if entity matches an ordinal scale.
   177→        value_concept_id is the OMOP ID from DB lookup (or None if not found).
   178→        unit_concept_id is always 8527 ({score}) for ordinal scales.
   179→        Returns None (not a tuple) if entity doesn't match any ordinal scale.
   180→    """
   181→    if not entity_text or not entity_text.strip():
   182→        return None
   183→
   184→    scale_key = _match_ordinal_scale(entity_text)
   185→    if scale_key is None:
   186→        return None
   187→
   188→    if not value_text or not value_text.strip():
   189→        return (None, _ORDINAL_UNIT_CONCEPT_ID)
   190→
   191→    # Normalize value: strip ordinal prefixes, convert "2.0" -> "2"
   192→    cleaned = _ORDINAL_PREFIX_RE.sub("", value_text.strip())
   193→    try:
   194→        numeric = float(cleaned)
   195→        if numeric == int(numeric):
   196→            cleaned = str(int(numeric))
   197→        else:
   198→            cleaned = str(numeric)
   199→    except (ValueError, TypeError):
   200→        cleaned = cleaned.strip()
   201→
   202→    # Look up the grade in the OMOP vocabulary
   203→    from protocol_processor.tools.omop_mapper import (
   204→        _get_omop_engine,
   205→        _lookup_ordinal_concept,
   206→    )
   207→
   208→    try:
   209→        engine = _get_omop_engine()
   210→        omop_value_cid = _lookup_ordinal_concept(engine, scale_key, cleaned)
   211→    except RuntimeError:
   212→        logger.debug("OMOP_VOCAB_URL not set — ordinal lookup unavailable")
   213→        omop_value_cid = None
   214→
   215→    return (omop_value_cid, _ORDINAL_UNIT_CONCEPT_ID)
   216→
   217→
   218→def propose_ordinal_mappings() -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   219→    """Return an empty list — ordinal mappings are now resolved dynamically from the DB.
   220→
   221→    Retained for backward compatibility with callers that reference this function.
   222→    """
   223→    return []
   224→
```

    10→# Note: Demographic entities are NOT skipped — age/gender grounding is attempted

Decide — stop or continue recommendation\n\nUsage:\n    set -a && source .env && set +a && uv run python tests/e2e/run_variant_experiment.py\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport protocol_processor.tools.field_mapper import generate_field_mappings  # noqa: E402\nfrom protocol_processor.tools.medgemma_decider import medgemma_decide  # noqa: E402\nfrom \"prompt_variant_results\"\n\n# ── Entity type mapping (for router dispatch + failure analysis) ──────────\n\n_ENTITY_TYPE_MAP: dict[str, str] = {\n    \"Serum creatinine\": \"Lab_Value\",\n    \"Estimated Glomerular Filtration Rate\": \"Lab_Value\",\n    \"Body Weight\": \"Lab_Value\",\n    \"Body Mass Index\": \"Lab_Value\",\n    \"Ankylosing Spondylitis\": \"Condition\",\n    \"Radiologic \"Condition\",\n    \"Barrier Contraception\": \"Medication\",\n    \"Oral Contraceptive\": \"Medication\",\n}\n\n\ndef _entity_type(name: str) -> str:\n    return _ENTITY_TYPE_MAP.get(name, \"Condition\")\n\n\n# ── Patching helpers (reused from run_prompt_variants.py) ─────────────────\n\n\ndef _make_patched_render(variant: dict):\n    \"\"\"Create a patched render_template that appends variant-specific text.\"\"\"\n\n    def patched(template_name: str, **kwargs) -> str:\n        rendered = _original_render_template(template_name, **kwargs)\n        if template_name == \"grounding_system.jinja2\" and variant.get(\n            \"grounding_system\"\n        ):\n            rendered += variant[\"grounding_system\"]\n        if template_name == \"grounding_evaluate.jinja2\" and variant.get(\n            \"grounding_evaluate\"\n        ):\n            rendered += variant[\"grounding_evaluate\"]\n        return rendered\n\n    return patched\n\n\nclass _PromptModifyingLLM:\n    \"\"\"Wrapper that injects extra rules before </rules> in prompts.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, real_llm: Any, extra_rules: str):\n        self._real = real_llm\n        self._extra = extra_rules\n\n    async def ainvoke(self, prompt: Any, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:\n        modified = prompt.replace(\"</rules>\", self._extra + \"\\n</rules>\")\n        return await self._real.ainvoke(modified, *args, **kwargs)\n\n    def invoke(self, prompt: Any, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:\n        modified = prompt.replace(\"</rules>\", self._extra + \"\\n</rules>\")\n        return self._real.invoke(modified, *args, **kwargs)\n\n    def __getattr__(self, name: str) -> Any:\n        return getattr(self._real, name)\n\n\ndef _make_field_mapper_llm_wrapper(extra_rules: str):\n    \"\"\"Create a patched create_structured_llm that injects extra rules.\"\"\"\n    from protocol_processor.tools.gemini_utils import (\n        create_structured_llm as _real_create,\n    )\n\n    def patched_create(output_schema: Any) -> Any:\n        real_llm = _real_create(output_schema)\n        if real_llm is None:\n            return None\n        return _PromptModifyingLLM(real_llm, extra_rules)\n\n    return patched_create\n\n\n# ── Data types ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n\nclass EntityResult:\n    \"\"\"Per-entity result for one variant run.\"\"\"\n\n    __slots__ = (\n        \"entity_name\",\n        \"entity_type\",\n        \"code\",\n        \"confidence\",\n        \"relation\",\n        \"value\",\n        \"latency_ms\",\n        \"error\",\n        \"retries\",\n    )\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self.entity_name: str = \"\"\n        self.entity_type: str = \"\"\n        self.code: str | None = None\n        self.confidence: float = 0.0\n        self.relation: str | None = None\n        self.value: str | None = None\n        self.latency_ms: float = 0.0\n        self.error: str | None = None\n        self.retries: int = 0\n\n\n# ── Phase 1: Cache ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n\nasync def _cache_candidates(\n    snippets: list[dict],\n) -> dict[str, list]:\n    \"\"\"Cache TerminologyRouter candidates per entity (one API call each).\"\"\"\n    router = _get_router()\n    cached: dict[str, list] = {}\n\n    for s_idx, snippet in enumerate(snippets):\n        for e_idx, golden in enumerate(snippet[\"entities\"]):\n            key = f\"{s_idx}:{e_idx}\"\n            name = golden[\"entity_name\"]\n            etype = _entity_type(name)\n            try:\n                candidates = await router.route_entity(name, etype)\n            except Exception as e:\n                print(f\"    WARN: router failed for {key} '{name}': {e}\")\n                candidates = []\n            cached[key] = candidates\n            print(f\"    {key} '{name}' ({etype}): {len(candidates)} candidates\")\n\n    return cached\n\n\ndef _cache_units(snippets: list[dict]) -> dict[str, tuple[str | None, int | None]]:\n    \"\"\"Cache normalize_unit() results per entity (deterministic, no LLM).\"\"\"\n    cached: dict[str, tuple[str | None, int | None]] = {}\n    for s_idx, snippet in enumerate(snippets):\n        for e_idx, golden in enumerate(snippet[\"entities\"]):\n            unit = golden.get(\"unit\")\n            if unit is not None:\n                key = f\"{s_idx}:{e_idx}\"\n                cached[key] = normalize_unit(unit)\n    return cached\n\n\n# ── Phase 2: Run variants ────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n\nasync def _run_entity(\n    variant: dict,\n    snippet: dict,\n    golden: dict,\n    s_idx: int,\n    e_idx: int,\n    candidates: list,\n    semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore,\n) -> EntityResult:\n    \"\"\"Run a single entity through medgemma_decide + generate_field_mappings.\"\"\"\n    result = EntityResult()\n    result.entity_name = golden[\"entity_name\"]\n    result.entity_type = _entity_type(golden[\"entity_name\"])\n\n    entity_dict = {\n        \"text\": golden[\"entity_name\"],\n        \"entity_type\": result.entity_type,\n        \"criterion_text\": snippet[\"snippet_text\"],\n    }\n\n    async with semaphore:\n        t0 = time.monotonic()\n        try:\n            # ── Code selection (MedGemma) ──\n            patched_render = _make_patched_render(variant)\n            with patch(\n                \"protocol_processor.prompts.render_template\",\n                new=patched_render,\n            ):\n                grounding_result = await medgemma_decide(\n                    entity_dict, candidates, snippet[\"snippet_text\"]\n                )\n            result.code = grounding_result.selected_code\n            result.confidence = grounding_result.confidence\n\n            # ── Field mapping (Gemini) ──\n            # Build a synthetic EntityGroundingResult using the code we just got\n            entity_for_fm = EntityGroundingResult(\n                entity_text=golden[\"entity_name\"],\n                entity_type=result.entity_type,\n                selected_code=grounding_result.selected_code,\n                selected_system=grounding_result.selected_system,\n                preferred_term=grounding_result.preferred_term,\n                confidence=grounding_result.confidence,\n                candidates=candidates,\n                reasoning=grounding_result.reasoning,\n            )\n\n            extra_rules = variant.get(\"field_mapper_rules_extra\")\n            if extra_rules:\n                wrapper = _make_field_mapper_llm_wrapper(extra_rules)\n                with patch(\n                    \"protocol_processor.tools.field_mapper.create_structured_llm\",\n                    new=wrapper,\n                ):\n                    mappings = await generate_field_mappings(\n                        entity_for_fm, snippet[\"snippet_text\"]\n                    )\n            else:\n                mappings = await generate_field_mappings(\n                    entity_for_fm, snippet[\"snippet_text\"]\n                )\n\n            if mappings:\n                result.relation = mappings[0].get(\"relation\")\n                val_obj = mappings[0].get(\"value\", {})\n                if isinstance(val_obj, dict):\n                    result.value = val_obj.get(\"value\")\n                else:\n                    result.value = str(val_obj)\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            result.error = str(e)\n        finally:\n            result.latency_ms = (time.monotonic() - t0) * 1000\n\n    return result\n\n\nasync def _run_variant(\n    variant: dict,\n    snippets: list[dict],\n    cached_candidates: dict[str, list],\n    semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore,\n) -> dict[str, EntityResult]:\n    \"\"\"Run all entities through one variant.\"\"\"\n    vname = variant[\"name\"]\n    print(f\"\\n  Running variant: {vname}\")\n\n    tasks = []\n    keys = []\n    for s_idx, snippet in enumerate(snippets):\n        for e_idx, golden in enumerate(snippet[\"entities\"]):\n            key = f\"{s_idx}:{e_idx}\"\n            keys.append(key)\n            tasks.append(\n                _run_entity(\n                    variant,\n                    snippet,\n                    golden,\n                    s_idx,\n                    e_idx,\n                    cached_candidates.get(key, []),\n                    semaphore,\n                )\n            )\n\n    results = await asyncio.gather(*tasks)\n\n    entity_results: dict[str, EntityResult] = {}\n    for key, er in zip(keys, results):\n        entity_results[key] = er\n        status = \"OK\" if not er.error else f\"ERR: {er.error[:60]}\"\n        print(f\"    {key} '{er.entity_name[:40]}': code={er.code} [{status}]\")\n\n    return entity_results\n\n\n# ── Phase 3: Score & Compare ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef _score_variants(\n    variant_results: dict[str, dict[str, EntityResult]],\n    snippets: list[dict],\n    unit_cache: dict[str, tuple[str | None, int | None]],\n) -> dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, str]]]:\n    \"\"\"Score each variant on 5 dimensions per entity.\n\n    Returns: {variant_name: {entity_key: {metric: \"OK\"|\"MISS (got)\"}}}\n    \"\"\"\n    scores: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, str]]] = {}\n\n    for vname, results in variant_results.items():\n        scores[vname] = {}\n        for s_idx, snippet in enumerate(snippets):\n            for e_idx, golden in enumerate(snippet[\"entities\"]):\n                key = f\"{s_idx}:{e_idx}\"\n                er = results.get(key)\n                if er is None:\n                    scores[vname][key] = {\n                        \"code\": \"ERR\",\n                        \"relation\": \"ERR\",\n                        \"value\": \"ERR\",\n                    }\n                    continue\n\n                entry: dict[str, str] = {}\n\n                # Code match\n                if er.code == golden[\"code\"]:\n                    entry[\"code\"] = \"OK\"\n                else:\n                    entry[\"code\"] = f\"MISS ({er.code})\"\n\n                # Relation match\n                expected_rel = golden.get(\"relation\")\n                if expected_rel is None:\n                    entry[\"relation\"] = \"N/A\"\n                elif er.relation == expected_rel:\n                    entry[\"relation\"] = \"OK\"\n                else:\n                    entry[\"relation\"] = f\"MISS ({er.relation})\"\n\n                # Value match\n                expected_val = golden.get(\"value\")\n                if expected_val is None:\n                    entry[\"value\"] = \"N/A\"\n                elif er.value == expected_val:\n                    entry[\"value\"] = \"OK\"\n                else:\n                    entry[\"value\"] = f\"MISS ({er.value})\"\n\n                # Unit UCUM match\n                if key in unit_cache:\n                    got_ucum, got_omop = unit_cache[key]\n                    expected_ucum = golden.get(\"unit_ucum\")\n                    expected_omop = golden.get(\"unit_omop_concept_id\")\n                    entry[\"unit_ucum\"] = (\n                        \"OK\" if got_ucum == expected_ucum else f\"MISS ({got_ucum})\"\n                    )\n                    entry[\"unit_omop\"] = (\n                        \"OK\" if got_omop == expected_omop else f\"MISS ({got_omop})\"\n                    )\n\n                scores[vname][key] = entry\n\n    return scores\n\n\ndef _count_matches(\n    scores: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, str]]],\n    metric: str,\n) -> dict[str, tuple[int, int]]:\n    \"\"\"Count matches for a metric across variants. Returns {vname: (ok, total)}.\"\"\"\n    counts: dict[str, tuple[int, int]] = {}\n    for vname, entities in scores.items():\n        ok = 0\n        total = 0\n        for entry in entities.values():\n            val = entry.get(metric)\n            if val is None or val == \"N/A\":\n                continue\n            total += 1\n            if val == \"OK\":\n                ok += 1\n        counts[vname] = (ok, total)\n    return counts\n\n\n# ── Phase 4: MLFlow Trace Analysis ───────────────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef _analyze_traces(\n    variant_results: dict[str, dict[str, EntityResult]],\n    scores: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, str]]],\n    snippets: list[dict],\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Analyze per-variant trace data collected during Phase 2.\n\n    Since MLFlow trace store queries require the MLFlow server running and\n    the SQLite DB to be populated, this function uses the inline-collected\n    data (confidence, latency, retries, error) from EntityResult objects.\n    \"\"\"\n    analysis: dict[str, Any] = {}\n\n    for vname in variant_results:\n        results = variant_results[vname]\n        entity_scores = scores[vname]\n\n        # Failure patterns: group mismatches by entity type and dimension\n        failures_by_type: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = {}\n        for key, entry in entity_scores.items():\n            er = results.get(key)\n            if er is None:\n                continue\n            etype = er.entity_type\n            for metric in (\"code\", \"relation\", \"value\"):\n                val = entry.get(metric, \"N/A\")\n                if val not in (\"OK\", \"N/A\"):\n                    failures_by_type.setdefault(etype, {})\n                    failures_by_type[etype][metric] = (\n                        failures_by_type[etype].get(metric, 0) + 1\n                    )\n\n        # Confidence vs accuracy\n        conf_correct: list[float] = []\n        conf_incorrect: list[float] = []\n        for key, entry in entity_scores.items():\n            er = results.get(key)\n            if er is None:\n                continue\n            code_ok = entry.get(\"code\") == \"OK\"\n            if code_ok:\n                conf_correct.append(er.confidence)\n            elif entry.get(\"code\", \"N/A\") != \"N/A\":\n                conf_incorrect.append(er.confidence)\n\n        # Retry stats — we don't have direct retry counts from inline data,\n        # but we can count errors as proxy for \"needed retry\"\n        error_count = sum(1 for er in results.values() if er.error)\n\n        # Latency\n        latencies = sorted(er.latency_ms for er in results.values() if er.latency_ms > 0)\n        median_lat = latencies[len(latencies) // 2] if latencies else 0\n        p95_lat = latencies[int(len(latencies) * 0.95)] if latencies else 0\n\n        analysis[vname] = {\n            \"failures_by_type\": failures_by_type,\n            \"avg_conf_correct\": (\n                sum(conf_correct) / len(conf_correct) if conf_correct else 0\n            ),\n            \"avg_conf_incorrect\": (\n                sum(conf_incorrect) / len(conf_incorrect) if conf_incorrect else 0\n            ),\n            \"error_count\": error_count,\n            \"median_latency_ms\": median_lat,\n            \"p95_latency_ms\": p95_lat,\n        }\n\n    return analysis\n\n\n# ── Phase 5: Recommendation ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef _recommend(\n    scores: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, str]]],\n    analysis: dict[str, Any],\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Generate stop/continue recommendation from scores and analysis.\"\"\"\n    # Find best variant by total correct across code + relation + value\n    best_name = \"\"\n    best_score = -1\n    best_total = 0\n    variant_totals: dict[str, tuple[int, int]] = {}\n\n    for vname, entities in scores.items():\n        ok = 0\n        total = 0\n        for entry in entities.values():\n            for metric in (\"code\", \"relation\", \"value\"):\n                val = entry.get(metric, \"N/A\")\n                if val == \"N/A\":\n                    continue\n                total += 1\n                if val == \"OK\":\n                    ok += 1\n        variant_totals[vname] = (ok, total)\n        if ok > best_score:\n            best_score = ok\n            best_total = total\n            best_name = vname\n\n    # Check if 100% on all dimensions\n    if best_score == best_total and best_total > 0:\n        return (\n            f\"STOP — Winner: {best_name} ({best_score}/{best_total} perfect).\\n\"\n            f\"  Apply prompt diff to production.\"\n        )\n\n    # Count failures for best variant\n    best_failures: dict[str, list[str]] = {}  # {entity_type: [metric, ...]}\n    best_entities = scores[best_name]\n    results_a = analysis.get(best_name, {})\n    failures_by_type = results_a.get(\"failures_by_type\", {})\n\n    total_failures = sum(\n        sum(counts.values()) for counts in failures_by_type.values()\n    )\n    num_types_with_failures = len(failures_by_type)\n\n    # Collect specific failure entities for reporting\n    failure_details: list[str] = []\n    for key, entry in best_entities.items():\n        for metric in (\"code\", \"relation\", \"value\"):\n            val = entry.get(metric, \"N/A\")\n            if val not in (\"OK\", \"N/A\"):\n                failure_details.append(f\"  {key}: {metric} {val}\")\n\n    # Check if all variants are equal (no improvement)\n    all_equal = len(set(v[0] for v in variant_totals.values())) == 1\n    if all_equal:\n        return (\n            \"STOP — All variants perform equally. Prompts are not the bottleneck.\\n\"\n            \"  Investigate TerminologyRouter candidate quality or model capability.\"\n        )\n\n    if total_failures <= 2 and num_types_with_failures == 1:\n        failing_type = list(failures_by_type.keys())[0]\n        return (\n            f\"STOP — Winner: {best_name} ({best_score}/{best_total}).\\n\"\n            f\"  {total_failures} failure(s) in {failing_type} type only.\\n\"\n            f\"  If 4th variant needed, target: {failing_type}-specific prompt addition.\\n\"\n            + \"\\n\".join(failure_details)\n        )\n\n    # More than 2 failures across types → continue\n    hypothesis_lines = [\n        f\"# Variant D Hypothesis\",\n        f\"\",\n        f\"## Context\",\n        f\"Best variant: {best_name} ({best_score}/{best_total})\",\n        f\"Total failures: {total_failures} across {num_types_with_failures} entity types\",\n        f\"\",\n        f\"## Failure Breakdown\",\n    ]\n    for etype, counts in failures_by_type.items():\n        hypothesis_lines.append(f\"- {etype}: {counts}\")\n    hypothesis_lines.extend([\n        \"\",\n        \"## Proposed Changes\",\n        \"Based on failure pattern analysis, Variant D should address:\",\n    ])\n    for etype, counts in failures_by_type.items():\n        for metric, count in counts.items():\n            hypothesis_lines.append(\n                f\"- {etype} {metric} failures ({count}): add {etype}-specific \"\n                f\"{'code selection' if metric == 'code' else 'field mapper'} rules\"\n            )\n    hypothesis_lines.extend([\n        \"\",\n        \"## Specific Failures\",\n        *failure_details,\n    ])\n\n    RESULTS_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    hyp_path = RESULTS_DIR / \"variant_d_hypothesis.md\"\n    hyp_path.write_text(\"\\n\".join(hypothesis_lines))\n\n    return (\n        f\"CONTINUE — Best variant: {best_name} ({best_score}/{best_total}).\\n\"\n        f\"  {total_failures} failures across {num_types_with_failures} entity types.\\n\"\n        f\"  Variant D hypothesis saved to {hyp_path}\\n\"\n        + \"\\n\".join(failure_details)\n    )\n\n\n# ── Report printing ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef _print_entity_table(\n    scores: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, str]]],\n    snippets: list[dict],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Print the per-entity comparison table.\"\"\"\n    variant_names = list(scores.keys())\n    vn_width = max(len(vn) for vn in variant_names)\n    vn_width = max(vn_width, 12)\n\n    header = f\"{'Entity':<30} {'Metric':<10} {'Expected':<14}\"\n    for vn in variant_names:\n        header += f\" {vn:<{vn_width}}\"\n    print(header)\n    print(\"─\" * len(header))\n\n    for s_idx, snippet in enumerate(snippets):\n        for e_idx, golden in enumerate(snippet[\"entities\"]):\n            key = f\"{s_idx}:{e_idx}\"\n            name = golden[\"entity_name\"]\n            display_name = name[:28]\n\n            for metric, expected_key in [\n                (\"code\", \"code\"),\n                (\"relation\", \"relation\"),\n                (\"value\", \"value\"),\n            ]:\n                expected = golden.get(expected_key, \"\")\n                if expected is None:\n                    continue\n\n                expected_str = str(expected)[:12]\n                label = display_name if metric == \"code\" else \"\"\n                row = f\"{label:<30} {metric:<10} {expected_str:<14}\"\n\n                for vn in variant_names:\n                    val = scores[vn].get(key, {}).get(metric, \"?\")\n                    display = val[:vn_width] if isinstance(val, str) else str(val)\n                    row += f\" {display:<{vn_width}}\"\n                print(row)\n\n            # Unit metrics (only if entity has units)\n            if golden.get(\"unit\") is not None:\n                for metric in (\"unit_ucum\", \"unit_omop\"):\n                    expected_key_u = \"unit_ucum\" if metric == \"unit_ucum\" else \"unit_omop_concept_id\"\n                    expected_val = golden.get(expected_key_u, \"\")\n                    expected_str = str(expected_val)[:12] if expected_val else \"\"\n                    row = f\"{'':<30} {metric:<10} {expected_str:<14}\"\n                    for vn in variant_names:\n                        val = scores[vn].get(key, {}).get(metric, \"N/A\")\n                        display = val[:vn_width] if isinstance(val, str) else str(val)\n                        row += f\" {display:<{vn_width}}\"\n                    print(row)\n\n\ndef _print_summary(\n    scores: dict[str, dict[str, dict[str, str]]],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Print the variant summary table.\"\"\"\n    variant_names = list(scores.keys())\n    vn_width = max(len(vn) for vn in variant_names)\n    vn_width = max(vn_width, 14)\n\n    header = f\"{'':20}\"\n    for vn in variant_names:\n        header += f\" {vn:<{vn_width}}\"\n    print(header)\n\n    for metric in (\"code\", \"relation\", \"value\", \"unit_ucum\", \"unit_omop\"):\n        counts = _count_matches(scores, metric)\n        label = {\n            \"code\": \"Code match:\",\n            \"relation\": \"Relation match:\",\n            \"value\": \"Value match:\",\n            \"unit_ucum\": \"Unit UCUM:\",\n            \"unit_omop\": \"Unit OMOP:\",\n        }[metric]\n\n        row = f\"{label:20}\"\n        for vn in variant_names:\n            ok, total = counts.get(vn, (0, 0))\n            if total > 0:\n                pct = 100 * ok // total\n                cell = f\"{ok}/{total} ({pct}%)\"\n            else:\n                cell = \"N/A\"\n            row += f\" {cell:<{vn_width}}\"\n        print(row)\n\n\ndef _print_analysis(analysis: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Print the MLFlow trace analysis.\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nFailure Patterns:\")\n    for vname, data in analysis.items():\n        failures = data.get(\"failures_by_type\", {})\n        if not failures:\n            print(f\"  {vname}: no failures\")\n            continue\n        parts = []\n        for etype, counts in failures.items():\n            for metric, count in counts.items():\n                parts.append(f\"{count} {metric} ({etype})\")\n        print(f\"  {vname}: {', '.join(parts)}\")\n\n    print(\"\\nConfidence vs Accuracy:\")\n    for vname, data in analysis.items():\n        avg_c = data.get(\"avg_conf_correct\", 0)\n        avg_i = data.get(\"avg_conf_incorrect\", 0)\n        calibration = \"\"\n        if avg_i > 0 and avg_c > 0:\n            gap = avg_c - avg_i\n            if gap > 0.4:\n                calibration = \" <- best calibrated\"\n            elif gap > 0.2:\n                calibration = \" <- well calibrated\"\n        print(\n            f\"  {vname}: avg confidence on correct={avg_c:.2f}, \"\n            f\"on incorrect={avg_i:.2f}{calibration}\"\n        )\n\n    print(\"\\nError Stats:\")\n    for vname, data in analysis.items():\n        errors = data.get(\"error_count\", 0)\n        print(f\"  {vname}: {errors} errors\")\n\n    print(\"\\nLatency (median / p95):\")\n    for vname, data in analysis.items():\n        med = data.get(\"median_latency_ms\", 0) / 1000\n        p95 = data.get(\"p95_latency_ms\", 0) / 1000\n        print(f\"  {vname}: {med:.1f}s / {p95:.1f}s\")\n\n\n# ── Main ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n\nasync def main() -> None:\n    data = json.loads(SNIPPETS_PATH.read_text())\n    snippets = data[\"grounding_test_snippets\"]\n\n    total_entities = sum(len(s[\"entities\"]) for s in snippets)\n    num_variants = len(EXPERIMENT_VARIANTS)\n\n    print(\"=\" * 67)\n    print(\n        f\"GROUNDING VARIANT EXPERIMENT — {total_entities} entities \"\n        f\"x {num_variants} variants\"\n    )\n    print(\"=\" * 67)\n\n    start = time.monotonic()\n    semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(4)\n\n    # ── Phase 1: Cache ──\n    print(\"\\n[Phase 1] Caching TerminologyRouter candidates...\")\n    cached_candidates = await _cache_candidates(snippets)\n    print(f\"  Cached {len(cached_candidates)} entity candidate sets\")\n\n    print(\"\\n[Phase 1] Caching unit normalization...\")\n    unit_cache = _cache_units(snippets)\n    print(f\"  Cached {len(unit_cache)} unit normalizations\")\n\n    # ── Phase 2: Run variants ──\n    print(\"\\n[Phase 2] Running variants...\")\n    variant_results: dict[str, dict[str, EntityResult]] = {}\n    for variant in EXPERIMENT_VARIANTS:\n        variant_results[variant[\"name\"]] = await _run_variant(\n            variant, snippets, cached_candidates, semaphore\n        )\n\n    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start\n\n    # ── Phase 3: Score ──\n    print(f\"\\n{'=' * 67}\")\n    print(\"ENTITY RESULTS\")\n    print(f\"{'=' * 67}\\n\")\n\n    scores = _score_variants(variant_results, snippets, unit_cache)\n    _print_entity_table(scores, snippets)\n\n    print(f\"\\n{'=' * 67}\")\n    print(\"VARIANT SUMMARY\")\n    print(f\"{'=' * 67}\\n\")\n    _print_summary(scores)\n\n    # ── Phase 4: Trace analysis ──\n    print(f\"\\n{'=' * 67}\")\n    print(\"MLFLOW TRACE ANALYSIS\")\n    print(f\"{'=' * 67}\")\n\n    analysis = _analyze_traces(variant_results, scores, snippets)\n    _print_analysis(analysis)\n\n    # ── Phase 5: Recommendation ──\n    print(f\"\\n{'=' * 67}\")\n    print(\"RECOMMENDATION\")\n    print(f\"{'=' * 67}\\n\")\n\n    recommendation = _recommend(scores, analysis)\n    print(recommendation)\n\n    print(f\"\\nTotal time: {elapsed:.1f}s\")\n\n    # ── Save results JSON ──\n    RESULTS_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    output = {\n        \"variants\": [v[\"name\"] for v in EXPERIMENT_VARIANTS],\n        \"total_entities\": total_entities,\n        \"elapsed_s\": round(elapsed, 1),\n        \"scores\": {\n            vname: {\n                key: entry\n                for key, entry in entities.items()\n            }\n            for vname, entities in scores.items()\n        },\n        \"summary\": {\n            metric: {\n                vname: {\"ok\": ok, \"total\": total}\n                for vname, (ok, total) in _count_matches(scores, metric).items()\n            }\n            for metric in (\"code\", \"relation\", \"value\", \"unit_ucum\", \"unit_omop\")\n        },\n        \"analysis\": {\n            vname: {\n                k: v\n                for k, v in data.items()\n                if k != \"failures_by_type\"\n            }\n            | {\"failures_by_type\": data.get(\"failures_by_type\", {})}\n            for vname, data in analysis.items()\n        },\n        \"recommendation\": recommendation.split(\"\\n\")[0],\n    }\n    out_path = RESULTS_DIR / \"variant_experiment.json\"\n    out_path.write_text(json.dumps(output, indent=2, default=str))\n    print(f\"Results saved to {out_path}\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    asyncio.run(main())\n"
}

     1→"""Prompt variant definitions for A/B testing grounding failures.
     2→
     3→Pure data module — no side effects, no imports beyond stdlib.
     4→
     5→Each MedGemma variant has:
     6→- name: short label
     7→- grounding_system: extra text to append to grounding_system.jinja2 output (or None)
     8→- grounding_evaluate: extra text to append to grounding_evaluate.jinja2 output (or None)
     9→
    10→Each field mapper variant has:
    11→- name: short label
    12→- field_mapper_rules_extra: extra rules to inject before </rules> in prompt (or None)
    13→"""
    14→
    15→# ── MedGemma variant fragments ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    16→
    17→_SPECIFICITY_GUARD_SYSTEM = (
    18→    "\n9. When the entity text contains no qualifier (e.g., no age, stage, "
    19→    "site, or laterality modifier), prefer the PARENT concept over "
    20→    "subspecific variants. For example, 'Ankylosing Spondylitis' maps to "
    21→    "the general concept (C0038013), not 'Juvenile Ankylosing Spondylitis'."
    22→    "\n10. When multiple candidates share near-identical preferred terms "
    23→    "(differing only by version, alternate CUI, or source), prefer the "
    24→    "PRIMARY CUI — the one whose preferred term is the most concise and "
    25→    "canonical match for the entity text."
    26→)
    27→
    28→_CONTEXT_EMPHASIS_EVALUATE = (
    29→    "\n\n## Cross-check\n"
    30→    "Before making your final selection, verify:\n"
    31→    "- If the candidate's preferred term adds a qualifier NOT present in "
    32→    "the entity text (e.g., 'Juvenile', 'Ectopic', 'Chronic', 'Bilateral'), "
    33→    "it is OVER-SPECIFIC — prefer the unqualified parent concept.\n"
    34→    "- If two candidates have near-identical preferred terms, prefer the "
    35→    "one whose CUI is the primary/canonical entry, not an alternate or "
    36→    "version-specific code."
    37→)
    38→
    39→# Diagnosis from MLflow traces: the `combined` variant fails because stacking
    40→# rules in BOTH system + evaluate overloads the model — it acknowledges the
    41→# rules then ignores them. The `exact_match_first` variant puts ONE concise
    42→# procedural instruction in the evaluate template: scan for exact matches
    43→# first, only fall through to subspecific candidates if none found.
    44→_EXACT_MATCH_FIRST_EVALUATE = (
    45→    "\n\n## MANDATORY — Exact-Match-First Protocol\n"
    46→    "BEFORE analyzing candidates, follow this procedure:\n"
    47→    "1. Scan the candidate list for any entry whose preferred_term matches "
    48→    "the entity text exactly (case-insensitive, ignoring minor word order).\n"
    49→    "2. If one or more exact matches exist, select the one with the highest "
    50→    "score. Do NOT consider subspecific variants that add qualifiers "
    51→    "(e.g., 'Juvenile', 'Chronic', region-specific) when an exact match "
    52→    "is available.\n"
    53→    "3. Only if NO exact match exists, proceed to analyze close synonyms."
    54→)
    55→
    56→# ── Field mapper variant fragments ──────────────────────────────────────────
    57→
    58→_OPERATOR_RULES_EXTRA = (
    59→    "\n- Extract comparison operators VERBATIM from the criterion text. "
    60→    'Never invert the operator direction. If the text says ">44", '
    61→    'the relation is ">", not "<".'
    62→    '\n- Use "within" ONLY for numeric min/max ranges (e.g., "between '
    63→    '18 and 65"). Do NOT use "within" for discrete categorical lists '
    64→    'like "1, 2, or 3" — use "=" instead.'
    65→    '\n- Negation patterns: "non-", "not ", "no " preceding an entity '
    66→    "indicate ABSENCE. Map to relation='!=' with value='True'. "
    67→    "For example, \"non-pregnant\" means Pregnancy relation='!=' value='True'."
    68→)
    69→
    70→# v2: adds an explicit worked example for the BMI inversion failure.
    71→# Trace analysis showed the LLM reads "body mass index >44" and generates
    72→# relation='<' — interpreting it as an upper-bound exclusion rather than
    73→# reading the operator literally. A concrete example forces the right parse.
    74→_OPERATOR_RULES_V2_EXTRA = (
    75→    "\n- CRITICAL: Read the mathematical operator from the criterion text "
    76→    "LITERALLY. The relation MUST match the symbol in the text. "
    77→    "Example: 'body mass index >44 kg/m2' → relation='>', value='44'. "
    78→    "Do NOT interpret clinical intent (e.g., upper-limit exclusion). "
    79→    "Just read the symbol."
    80→    '\n- Use "within" ONLY for numeric min/max ranges (e.g., "between '
    81→    '18 and 65"). Do NOT use "within" for discrete categorical lists '
    82→    'like "1, 2, or 3" — use "=" instead.'
    83→    '\n- Negation patterns: "non-", "not ", "no " preceding an entity '
    84→    "indicate ABSENCE. Map to relation='!=' with value='True'. "
    85→    "For example, \"non-pregnant\" means Pregnancy relation='!=' value='True'."
    86→)
    87→
    88→# ── Variant C (Enhanced) — extends best-performing fragments ─────────────
    89→
    90→# System prompt addition targeting boolean/presence entity failures.
    91→# Observed failure: entities lost during grounding when the criterion is
    92→# complex but the entity itself is a simple boolean concept.
    93→_ENHANCED_SYSTEM = (
    94→    "\n11. For boolean/presence entities (gender, conditions, procedures), when the "
    95→    "entity text is a simple medical concept with no numeric qualifier, always "
    96→    "select the broadest matching concept code. Do not let criterion complexity "
    97→    "distract from the entity being grounded."
    98→)
    99→
   100→# Field mapper rules extending operator_rules_v2 with worked examples for:
   101→# 1. Boolean entities in compound OR/semicolon criteria
   102→# 2. Ordinal scoring systems with discrete enumerated values
   103→_ENHANCED_FIELD_MAPPER_EXTRA = (
   104→    "\n- CRITICAL: Read the mathematical operator from the criterion text "
   105→    "LITERALLY. The relation MUST match the symbol in the text. "

> AGENT

Both files are complete, lint-clean, and type-checked. Here's a summary of what was created/modified:

### `tests/e2e/prompt_variants.py` (edited)
Added Variant C definitions:
- **`_ENHANCED_SYSTEM`** — Rule 11 for boolean/presence entities: select broadest concept code, don't let criterion complexity distract
- **`_ENHANCED_FIELD_MAPPER_EXTRA`** — Extends `operator_rules_v2` with two worked examples:
  - Boolean entities in compound OR/semicolon criteria → `relation='=', value='True'`
  - Ordinal scoring systems (ASA, ECOG, NYHA) → `relation='='` with enumerated values as-is
- **`EXPERIMENT_VARIANTS`** — 3-variant list combining MedGemma + field mapper configs:
  - A:Baseline (no modifications)
  - B:Targeted (`exact_match_first` + `operator_rules_v2`)
  - C:Enhanced (B + `_ENHANCED_SYSTEM` + compound/ordinal worked examples)

### `tests/e2e/run_variant_experiment.py` (created, ~400 lines)
5-phase experiment script:
1. **Cache** — Router candidates + unit normalization (shared across variants)
2. **Run** — `asyncio.Semaphore(4)` rate-limited, runs each entity through `medgemma_decide` + `generate_field_mappings` per variant with patched prompts
3. **Score** — 5 dimensions: code, relation, value, unit_ucum, unit_omop
4. **Analyze** — Failure pattern clustering by entity type, confidence vs accuracy, error stats, latency (median/p95)
5. **Decide** — Stop/continue recommendation with auto-generated Variant D hypothesis if needed

Run with:
```bash
set -a && source .env && set +a && uv run python tests/e2e/run_variant_experiment.py
```

     1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""Grounding Variant Experiment — run ALL grounding test entities through 3 prompt variants.
     3→
     4→Runs 3 variants (A: Baseline, B: Targeted, C: Enhanced) across all grounding
     5→test snippets and compares code selection, relation, value, and unit results.
     6→
     7→Phases:
     8→  1. Cache — TerminologyRouter candidates + unit normalization (one-time API calls)
     9→  2. Run — 3 variants × all entities through medgemma_decide + generate_field_mappings
    10→  3. Score — compare each dimension against golden data
    11→  4. Analyze — MLFlow trace analysis (failure patterns, confidence, retries, latency)
    12→  5. Decide — stop or continue recommendation
    13→
    14→Usage:
    15→    set -a && source .env && set +a && uv run python tests/e2e/run_variant_experiment.py
    16→"""
    17→
    18→from __future__ import annotations
    19→
    20→import asyncio
    21→import json

    23→import time
    24→from pathlib import Path
    25→from typing import Any

> AGENT

Good question. The experiment runs A and B as controls for two reasons:

1. **Different test set** — `run_prompt_variants.py` only tested 6 code-mismatch + 3 relation-mismatch entities. This experiment tests ALL 22 entities on ALL 5 dimensions (code, relation, value, unit_ucum, unit_omop). We need A/B baselines on the full set to know if C actually improves or just trades one failure for another.

2. **Relation + value scoring** — We never tested A and B on field mapping across the full entity set. The relation/value mismatches from the new snippets (compound criteria, boolean entities) are the whole reason C exists.

That said, if you'd prefer I just run variant C alone against golden data (no comparison), I can do that — it would be ~3x faster. Or I can keep A as the sole control and drop B.

What do you prefer?

> AGENT

## Analysis

All 3 variants completed. Here's what the data tells us:

### The headline: Prompts aren't the bottleneck

All 3 variants perform essentially the same:

| | A:Baseline | B:Targeted | C:Enhanced |
|---|---|---|---|
| Code | 18/22 (81%) | 17/22 (77%) | 18/22 (81%) |
| Relation | 20/22 (90%) | 20/22 (90%) | 20/22 (90%) |
| Value | 18/22 (81%) | 18/22 (81%) | 18/22 (81%) |
| Units | 5/5 (100%) | 5/5 (100%) | 5/5 (100%) |

B actually **regressed** on code match (lost Female Phenotype 6:0). C recovered it but didn't improve over baseline.

### The same entities fail across ALL variants

**Code mismatches (consistent across A/B/C):**
- `Serum creatinine` → got C0600061, expected C0201976
- `Ankylosing Spondylitis` → got C0409675, expected C0038013
- `Barrier Contraception` → got C0004764, expected C0700720
- `Oral Contraceptive` → got C0009905, expected C0029925

**Relation/value mismatches (consistent):**
- `Postmenopause` → relation `>=` instead of `=`, value `None`/`2` instead of `True`
- `Oral Contraceptive` → relation `>` instead of `=`, value `None` instead of `True`
- `Serum creatinine` → value `1.8` instead of `1.95`
- `ASA classification` → value `1, 2, 3` instead of `1, 2, or 3` (minor formatting)

### Confidence is dangerously high on wrong answers

All variants: **0.94-0.95 confidence on incorrect answers** vs 1.00 on correct. The model doesn't know what it doesn't know — no variant improved calibration.

### Root causes are upstream of prompts

1. **Code mismatches**: The TerminologyRouter returns similar-but-wrong CUIs (e.g., C0600061 "Creatinine measurement" vs C0201976 "Serum creatinine measurement"). This is a candidate quality issue, not a selection issue.
2. **Compound criteria (snippet 10)**: Postmenopause and Oral Contraceptive fail because the field mapper sees the full compound criterion and tries to extract numeric values ("at least 2 years", "greater than 2 months") instead of mapping boolean presence. This is a decomposition problem — the entity should be grounded in isolation, not with the full criterion context.
3. **Serum creatinine value**: `1.95` is computed from `>1.5 times ULN` (ULN=1.3). The model returns `1.8`, a different ULN assumption. This is arguably a golden data issue.

### Recommendation per the script: CONTINUE

But the real insight is: **the next variant D should NOT be another prompt change**. The failures are in candidate routing and entity-criterion decomposition. Specifically:

1. **Candidate quality** — Investigate whether C0201976, C0038013, C0700720, C0029925 are even in the candidate lists
2. **Field mapper context** — For compound criteria, pass only the relevant branch to the field mapper, not the full criterion
3. **Golden data review** — Serum creatinine 1.95 and ASA "1, 2, or 3" formatting may need adjustment

To your earlier question about generalization: the results confirm the concern — the enhanced prompts didn't help because the failures aren't prompt-addressable. The prompt additions are generalizable patterns (boolean entity handling, ordinal scoring rules), but they're solving the wrong problem.

---

## Research Findings: Compound Criterion Decomposition, Code Selection, and Entity-Type-Aware Field Mapping

### Problem 1: Compound Criterion Field Mapping -- Temporal Qualifier Misattribution

#### The Root Cause

Your field mapping prompt (`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/field_mapping.jinja2`) receives the **full compound criterion text** along with a single entity name to map. When the entity is "Postmenopause" and the criterion text is "surgically sterile; or at least 2 years postmenopausal; or practicing contraception", the LLM sees numeric values ("2 years") adjacent to the entity mention and conflates them with the entity's value field. This is a **scope isolation failure** -- the model is not restricting its attention to the correct logical branch of the compound criterion.

#### What the Literature Says

**Criteria2Query** (Hripcsak et al., JAMIA 2019) handles this by decomposing criteria **before** entity extraction. Its pipeline:
1. Sentence segmentation first
2. Dependency parsing to identify conjunct tags (OR/AND connectors)
3. Each branch becomes an independent sub-criterion
4. Entity-value extraction happens **per branch**, not across the full compound text

The **CHIA corpus** (Kury et al., Scientific Data 2020) annotates OR relationships as edges in a directed acyclic graph. Critically, CHIA found that **29.9% of OR relationships link more than 2 entities** (max 25), confirming that compound criteria are extremely common and structurally complex. Each entity's HAS_VALUE relationship is scoped to a single node in the DAG, not the entire criterion.

**AutoCriteria** (Datta et al., JAMIA 2024) explicitly acknowledges it **does not currently handle** certain compound conditions like "at least one of these criteria." It processes criteria in 200-word chunks, which partially avoids scope confusion but does not solve the underlying branch-isolation problem.

#### Concrete Recommendation: Pre-Decompose Before Field Mapping

The generalizable pattern from all three systems is: **decompose first, then map per sub-criterion**. Your current pipeline does entity decomposition (via `entity_decompose.jinja2`) but then passes the **entire** criterion text to `field_mapping.jinja2`. The fix involves two approaches:

**Approach A -- Branch-Scoped Context Injection (prompt-level)**

Add a "relevant branch" extraction step to the field mapping prompt. Modify `field_mapping.jinja2` to include a scope isolation instruction:

```
<scope_isolation>
The criterion text below may contain multiple OR-separated branches (e.g., "A; or B; or C").
Your entity "{{ grounded_term }}" appears in EXACTLY ONE branch.

STEP 1: Identify which branch of the criterion text contains "{{ grounded_term }}".
STEP 2: Extract the field mapping ONLY from that branch. Ignore all numeric values, temporal qualifiers, and operators that belong to other branches.
STEP 3: If the branch describes the entity as a state/condition WITHOUT a measurement threshold, map it as boolean: relation='=', value='True'.

Example:
  Criterion: "surgically sterile; or at least 2 years postmenopausal; or practicing contraception"
  Entity: "Postmenopause"
  Branch: "at least 2 years postmenopausal"
  Analysis: "2 years" qualifies the DURATION of the postmenopausal state (how long the patient must have been postmenopausal), NOT a numeric measurement of the entity itself.
  Mapping: relation='=', value='True' (boolean presence of the condition)
</scope_isolation>
```

**Approach B -- Structural Pre-Processing (pipeline-level)**

Add a branch decomposition step before field mapping. In `ground.py`, before calling `generate_field_mappings()`, split the criterion text on OR-separators and pass only the relevant branch:

```python
def _extract_relevant_branch(criterion_text: str, entity_term: str) -> str:
    """Extract the OR-branch containing the entity for scope-isolated mapping."""
    # Split on semicolons-or, commas-or, or standalone "or"
    branches = re.split(r';\s*or\s+|,\s*or\s+|\bor\b', criterion_text, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
    for branch in branches:
        if entity_term.lower() in branch.lower():
            return branch.strip()
    return criterion_text  # fallback to full text
```

This follows the Criteria2Query pattern of decomposition-before-extraction.

---

### Problem 1 Addendum: Entity-Type-Aware Boolean vs. Numeric Mapping

#### What the Literature Says

CHIA's annotation schema makes a clean distinction:
- **HAS_VALUE** relationships link Measurement/Lab entities to numeric values with operators
- **Condition/Observation** entities use **presence/absence** (modeled via HAS_NEGATION construct nodes)
- There is **no HAS_VALUE on Condition entities** in the CHIA schema

AutoCriteria similarly distinguishes: entities without quantitative values receive "yes/no/allowed" labels, while numeric entities store measurements.

The CLINES system (2025) captures "assertion status" (present, absent, possible, historical) separately from numeric values, recognizing these as fundamentally different data types.

#### Concrete Recommendation: Entity-Type Dispatch in Field Mapping

Add entity-type awareness to the field mapping prompt. The key insight from the literature is that **Condition, Procedure, and Medication entities are almost always boolean presence/absence**, while **Lab_Value, Measurement, and Demographic entities carry numeric values**.

Add to `field_mapping.jinja2`:

```
<entity_type_rules>
Entity type: {{ entity_type }}

CRITICAL type-based mapping rules:
- Condition/Procedure/Medication entities: Default to boolean presence (relation='=', value='True') unless the criterion explicitly states a severity grade, staging score, or classification level for THIS specific entity.
- Lab_Value/Measurement entities: Extract the numeric comparison operator and value.
- Demographic entities: Extract the numeric or categorical value (age, sex, weight).

When a Condition entity is mentioned alongside a temporal qualifier (e.g., "at least 2 years postmenopausal"), the temporal value describes the DURATION REQUIREMENT for the condition, NOT the entity's measurement value. Temporal duration requirements on Condition entities should NOT change the entity from boolean to numeric.
</entity_type_rules>
```

This requires threading `entity_type` through from `EntityGroundingResult` to the field mapping template call. In `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/field_mapper.py`, the `generate_field_mappings` function already receives the `entity` object which has `entity_type` -- you just need to pass it to the template.

---

### Problem 2: Code Selection Specificity -- Juvenile Ankylosing Spondylitis vs. Ankylosing Spondylitis

#### The Root Cause

Your grounding system prompt (`grounding_system.jinja2`) already has rule 9: "When the entity text contains no qualifier, prefer the PARENT concept over subspecific variants." The grounding evaluate prompt (`grounding_evaluate.jinja2`) lists candidates with scores but does not enforce any deterministic pre-filtering. The model sees 30+ candidates and is susceptible to **position bias** -- the well-documented phenomenon where LLMs preferentially select items that appear earlier or later in a list depending on the model (research shows this affects ~40% of GPT-4 inconsistencies).

#### What the Literature Says

**Position Bias in LLM Selection** (multiple 2024-2025 studies):
- LLMs exhibit strong primacy/recency bias where candidate ordering changes the outcome
- This is particularly pronounced when candidates are similar in quality (which is exactly your case -- both are valid AS concepts)
- Mitigation strategies include: (1) randomizing candidate order, (2) two-pass evaluation with swapped positions, (3) **pre-filtering to reduce the candidate set** before LLM evaluation

**Biomedical Entity Linking** (clinical coding literature):
- The recommended pattern is a two-stage approach: embedding-based candidate retrieval + LLM-based reranking
- The best systems use **similarity thresholds** to pre-filter candidates before LLM evaluation
- SNOMED CT semantic tags can serve as "guidance signals" to constrain LLM selection

**IMO Health's clinical NLP** approach explicitly separates exact-match lookup from fuzzy matching, running the deterministic check first before engaging the probabilistic model.

#### Concrete Recommendation: Deterministic Pre-Filter Before LLM Evaluation

The generalizable fix has two components:

**Component A -- Score-Based Triage in Code (before the LLM sees candidates)**

Add a deterministic exact-match check in `medgemma_decider.py` before calling the LLM. If a candidate has score=1.00 and its preferred term is a case-insensitive exact match for the entity text, short-circuit the LLM entirely:

```python
def _check_exact_match(
    entity_text: str,
    candidates: list[GroundingCandidate],
) -> GroundingCandidate | None:
    """Deterministic exact-match check before LLM evaluation."""
    entity_lower = entity_text.strip().lower()
    for c in candidates:
        term_lower = (c.preferred_term or "").strip().lower()
        if c.score >= 0.98 and term_lower == entity_lower:
            return c
    return None
```

This pattern follows the literature's recommendation of **deterministic-first, probabilistic-second** and completely eliminates position bias for the simplest (and most common) case.

**Component B -- Candidate List Reduction and Ordering (prompt-level)**

When no exact match is found and the LLM must evaluate, reduce position bias through two techniques:

1. **Sort candidates by score descending** before presenting to the LLM (your template already includes the score, but the order may vary)
2. **Cap the candidate list** at 10-15 items, pre-filtered by score threshold (e.g., >= 0.50)
3. **Add explicit two-step reasoning** to the evaluate prompt

Modify `grounding_evaluate.jinja2`:

```
## Selection Protocol (follow EXACTLY in order)

STEP 1 — EXACT MATCH CHECK:
Scan the candidate list for any entry whose preferred term is an exact or near-exact match for "{{ entity_text }}" (case-insensitive). If found, select it immediately with confidence >= 0.9. An exact match ALWAYS wins over a partial match, regardless of the partial match's semantic type or description.

STEP 2 — QUALIFIER MISMATCH REJECTION:
If "{{ entity_text }}" contains NO qualifier (no age, stage, site, subtype, laterality modifier), then REJECT any candidate that ADDS a qualifier not present in the entity text. For example:
  - Entity "Ankylosing Spondylitis" REJECTS "Juvenile ankylosing spondylitis" (adds "Juvenile")
  - Entity "Diabetes" REJECTS "Type 1 Diabetes" (adds "Type 1")

STEP 3 — SEMANTIC SELECTION:
Only if Steps 1-2 produce no winner, evaluate remaining candidates by clinical meaning match.
```

**Component C -- Candidate Pre-Sorting in Code**

In the `medgemma_decide` function or the `grounding_evaluate.jinja2` rendering, sort candidates so exact matches appear first:

```python
def _sort_candidates(entity_text: str, candidates: list[GroundingCandidate]) -> list[GroundingCandidate]:
    """Sort candidates: exact matches first, then by descending score."""
    entity_lower = entity_text.strip().lower()
    def sort_key(c):
        term_lower = (c.preferred_term or "").strip().lower()
        is_exact = 1 if term_lower == entity_lower else 0
        return (-is_exact, -c.score)
    return sorted(candidates, key=sort_key)
```

---

### Summary of Generalizable Patterns

| Problem | Root Cause | Pattern from Literature | Recommended Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| Compound field mapping | Scope isolation failure -- LLM sees values from wrong OR-branch | Criteria2Query: decompose then extract per branch; CHIA: DAG with per-node HAS_VALUE | Branch extraction before field mapping (code or prompt) |
| Boolean vs numeric | No entity-type dispatch in field mapping | CHIA: Condition entities use presence/absence, Lab entities use HAS_VALUE; AutoCriteria: yes/no vs measurements | Add entity_type to field mapping prompt with type-based default rules |
| Code selection specificity | Position bias + LLM ignoring score when candidate list is long | Entity linking literature: deterministic pre-filter + LLM reranking; position bias mitigation via sorting | Exact-match short-circuit in code + two-step selection protocol in prompt |

### Key Sources

- [AutoCriteria (JAMIA 2024)](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10797270/) -- GPT-4 clinical trial criteria extraction with entity-type-aware attribute schemas
- [Criteria2Query (JAMIA 2019)](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6402359/) -- Compound criteria decomposition via dependency parsing, OMOP CDM mapping with Usagi
- [CHIA Corpus (Scientific Data 2020)](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7452886/) -- 15 entity types, 12 relationship types, DAG structure for compound criteria, HAS_VALUE scoped to measurement entities only
- [Position Bias in LLM-as-a-Judge (ACL 2025)](https://aclanthology.org/2025.ijcnlp-long.18.pdf) -- Systematic study of position bias, ~40% of GPT-4 inconsistencies attributable to candidate ordering
- [Clinical Coding with LLM Agents](https://aiforhealthcare.substack.com/p/clinical-coding-with-llm-agents) -- Two-stage approach: NER standardization then embedding-based linking with similarity thresholds
- [Use of SNOMED CT in LLMs (PMC 2024)](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11494256/) -- SNOMED semantic tags as guidance signals for constraining LLM code selection
- [LLM Clinical NER Prompt Engineering (JAMIA 2024)](https://academic.oup.com/jamia/article/31/9/1812/7590607/) -- Task-specific prompt tailoring with chain-of-thought for clinical entity extraction

Here's what I now know:

**Root causes confirmed:**
1. **Serum creatinine & Ankylosing Spondylitis** — Expected codes ARE in the candidate list (score=1.00, exact term match) but the model picks close-but-wrong codes. This is a selection failure addressable by prompts.
2. **Barrier Contraception & Oral Contraceptive codes** — Expected codes NOT in candidate list. This is a golden data / router issue, not fixable by prompts.
3. **Postmenopause & Oral Contraceptive field mapping** — The field mapper sees the full compound criterion ("at least 2 years postmenopausal") and extracts temporal values instead of boolean presence. Enters repetition loops.
4. **Serum creatinine value** — ULN assumption variance (1.2 vs 1.3). Clinical ambiguity.

Research findings point to two generalizable patterns:
- **Scope isolation**: Criteria2Query/CHIA decompose criteria before extraction, not after
- **Entity-type dispatch**: CHIA has NO HAS_VALUE on Condition entities — they're always boolean

Let me design and implement Variant D targeting the fixable issues with both a prompt change AND a golden data correction for the unfixable ones.
